{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01RCfkaTpjW9FXBegPfWQvkr","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# AGENTS.md\n2\t\n3\t## Context Loading Order\n4\t\n5\tAgents should read files in this order to understand the HAL Engine codebase:\n6\t\n7\t1. **README.md** - Overview, features, quick start, and project structure\n8\t2. **package.json** - Dependencies, scripts, tech stack validation\n9\t3. **tsconfig.json** - TypeScript configuration and compilation targets\n10\t4. **src/index.ts** - Public API exports and module surface\n11\t5. **src/config.ts** - Main factory function `createHalEngine()` and configuration schema\n12\t6. **src/types/** - Core type definitions (ai.ts, session.ts, messages.ts, auth.ts, sessionStore.ts)\n13\t7. **src/providers/** - AI provider implementations (bedrock, vertex, openai, anthropic, mock)\n14\t8. **src/infrastructure/** - Support layers (builders, stores, parsers)\n15\t9. **src/orchestration/** - Business logic (chatOrchestrator, tools, context management)\n16\t10. **src/transport/** - Server setup (Express app, WebSocket, routes)\n17\t11. **specs/** - Normative specs (architecture, WebSocket protocol, providers, tool responses)\n18\t12. **adrs/** - Architecture Decision Records\n19\t13. **docs/** - How-to guides, coding practices, and research spikes under docs/spikes/\n20\t14. **.specify/spec.md** - Repo-level system spec (exempt from the spec header table convention)\n21\t\n22\t## Workflow Commands\n23\t\n24\t### Development\n25\t```bash\n26\tnpm run dev          # Start example/server.ts with hot reload (tsx watch)\n27\tnpm run typecheck    # Run TypeScript type checking without emitting\n28\t```\n29\t\n30\t### Building\n31\t```bash\n32\tnpm run build        # Compile TypeScript to dist/ (tsc)\n33\tnpm start            # Run compiled JavaScript from dist/index.js\n34\t```\n35\t\n36\t### Testing\n37\t```bash\n38\tnpm test             # Run Jest test suite\n39\t# Tests use ts-jest for TypeScript compilation\n40\t# Configuration: jest.config.js\n41\t```\n42\t\n43\t### Code Quality\n44\t```bash\n45\tnpm run eslint       # Run ESLint with max-warnings 0 (zero-tolerance)\n46\tnpm run prettier     # Format all src/**/*.ts with Prettier\n47\tnpm run prettier:check  # Check formatting without writing\n48\t```\n49\t\n50\t### All Checks (Recommended Pre-Commit)\n51\t```bash\n52\tnpm run verify\n53\t```\n54\t\n55\tThat is the blocking set `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `verify` job runs on every\n56\tpull request, in the same order, and `scripts/verify-script.test.ts` fails if the\n57\ttwo stop agreeing - so this command cannot quietly become a subset of what CI\n58\tdemands. Two gates in that job are missing from it because both diff against\n59\t`origin/main` and neither runs the same way on a checkout: the spec anchor check\n60\tand the changelog check.\n61\t\n62\tTwo further CI jobs are worth running locally before a release or a change that\n63\ttouches packaging: `npm run check:build-chain` (audit and action pins) and\n64\t`npm run smoke` (packs the tarball and installs it outside this tree).\n65\t\n66\t## Spec Header Table\n67\t\n68\tEvery `specs/*/spec.md` opens with its title, then a two-column header table,\n69\tand carries a lead paragraph of prose before its first `##` section:\n70\t\n71\t```markdown\n72\t# <spec title>\n73\t\n74\t| Field  | Value                  |\n75\t| ------ | ---------------------- |\n76\t| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#<n> |\n77\t| Status | Draft                  |\n78\t\n79\t<lead paragraph>\n80\t```\n81\t\n82\tThe lead paragraph does not have to sit immediately after the table: a spec\n83\tcarrying a metadata block of its own may place that block between the two. What\n84\tthe convention requires is that prose, rather than a section heading, follows\n85\tthe header material, and that the prose runs to at least 40 characters -\n86\tanything shorter is reported as no lead paragraph at all.\n87\t\n88\t`Issue` names the issue the spec was written against, or `n/a` for the specs\n89\tthat predate this convention.\n90\t\n91\t`Status` is one of `Draft`, `In Progress` or `Shipped`. It is not a mood, and\n92\tit is not a claim about how finished the feature is: it is the spec's own\n93\ttest-citation coverage. No testable statement cited is `Draft`, some cited is\n94\t`In Progress`, all cited is `Shipped`. A spec describing shipped, working\n95\tbehaviour still reads `Draft` until its statements cite the tests that\n96\tvalidate them.\n97\t\n98\tA spec that should no longer be graded takes a terminal value instead. The\n99\tparser buckets roughly sixteen labels into five buckets, and the two terminal\n100\tones skip the coverage check whatever the citation count:\n101\t\n102\t| Written into the spec                                            | Bucket                       |\n103\t| ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |\n104\t| `Draft`                                                          | `draft`                      |\n105\t| `In Progress`, `In Review`, `Planning`, `WIP`, `Proposed`        | `in-progress`                |\n106\t| `Shipped`, `Implemented`, `Complete`, `Accepted`, `Done`, `Live` | `shipped`                    |\n107\t| `Retired`, `Superseded`, `Removed`, `Deprecated`, `Obsolete`     | `retired` - skips the check  |\n108\t| `Rejected`, `Abandoned`                                          | `rejected` - skips the check |\n109\t\n110\tPrefer the canonical `Draft` / `In Progress` / `Shipped` for a live spec; reach\n111\tfor `Retired` or `Rejected` rather than leaving a superseded spec to claim a\n112\tcoverage tier it will never earn.\n113\t\n114\tADRs do not carry this table. They declare their state as YAML frontmatter\n115\t`status:` instead - `accepted` for a decision the code implements, `proposed`\n116\tfor one it does not. That field does mean maturity, which the spec `Status` row\n117\texplicitly does not. The difference is deliberate: ADRs are exempt from the\n118\tcoverage tier, because asking a decision record to cite a test for every\n119\tstatement it makes is the wrong ask of a decision record.\n120\t\n121\t`.specify/spec.md` is exempt from this section. It is the repo-level system\n122\tspec, it predates the convention, and its content is deliberately frozen -\n123\tgiving it a header table and a lead paragraph would mean writing new prose into\n124\ta file that is meant to stay unchanged. The exemption covers the header table\n125\tand the lead paragraph only: the Lore coverage job does scan\n126\t`.specify/spec.md`, so link placement still applies to it.\n127\t\n128\t## Spec Test Links\n129\t\n130\tStatements in `specs/*/spec.md` cite their validating test in a trailing\n131\tparenthetical at the end of the statement, where a list item counts as one\n132\tstatement and a paragraph counts per sentence. Tests live beside their source\n133\tin this repo, so the path points into `src/`:\n134\t\n135\t```markdown\n136\t- All entries within a single assistant response are committed before the next\n137\t  user message is processed\n138\t  ([validated by: commits every entry before the next user message](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L42)).\n139\t```\n140\t\n141\tThe citation carries the test's **name** as well as its line. The name is what the\n142\tauthor cited; the line is a derived pointer, and it goes stale on its own - adding\n143\tan import above a suite moves every test below it, and the citation lands on a\n144\tdifferent, still-valid declaration without anything noticing. `npm run spec:names`\n145\tholds each citation to the test it names, and `npm run spec:names:fix` repoints the\n146\tline from the name. The `#Lnn` stays because the lint rule and the coverage job\n147\tboth index by it; a citation without one marks the whole file covered rather than\n148\tone test.\n149\t\n150\tA citation counts as coverage only where it is trailing, and a test link placed\n151\tanywhere else in the statement is a misplaced citation rather than a weaker one.\n152\tUpstream `check:spec-links` reports it and exits 1, and the coverage job that\n153\tconsumes these specs records nothing for it - so a spec can read as fully cited while its coverage is\n154\tzero. A mid-statement link to something that is not a test file - a script, a\n155\tdoc, another spec - is ordinary prose, is never reported, and was never coverage\n156\tto begin with. A misplaced citation is fixed by moving it to the end of its\n157\tstatement, never by deleting it.\n158\t\n159\tIn a test file the `#Lnn` anchor must land on the `it(`, `test(` or `describe(`\n160\tdeclaration itself - not a blank line, not one holding only closing punctuation,\n161\tand not a line inside the test body. A citation onto an `expect` renders and\n162\tresolves like any other, and proves nothing: no reader can turn that line back\n163\tinto the name of a case. `scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs` reports all four as\n164\t`rotten` and exits 1 in both modes. A citation into a file that is not a test -\n165\ta workflow, a config, `package.json` - has no declaration to land on and is held\n166\tonly to existing and carrying content.\n167\t\n168\tWhat no gate can catch is an anchor aimed at the wrong declaration: a real but\n169\tunintended `it(` passes every check. The tell is that the test the statement\n170\tmeant to cite goes on reading as uncited in the `require-spec-link` backlog.\n171\t\n172\tAnchors drift silently whenever a cited file gains or loses lines above them, so\n173\tafter editing any cited file re-run `node scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs`\n174\trather than hand-correcting the numbers. That script cannot repair a spec absent\n175\tfrom the base ref, since it has no baseline to translate from; on the branch that\n176\tintroduces a spec, its anchors have to be corrected by hand.\n177\t\n178\tThe convention's tooling is wired up here: `npm run check:spec-links`, `npm run check:spec-status` and\n179\t`node scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs --check origin/main` all gate CI, and\n180\t`re-lint/require-status-matches-coverage` derives each `Status` row from its\n181\tspec's citations. `re-lint/require-spec-link` gates in `eslint.config.mjs`: every test cites a\n182\tstatement, so an uncited new test fails the build. Its statement-side mirror,\n183\t`require-statement-links`, runs at `warn` from `eslint.config.backlog.mjs` and\n184\treports without blocking - the severities the rule pair is designed for.\n185\t\n186\t## Commit Conventions\n187\t\n188\t### Format\n189\tFollow conventional commits:\n190\t```\n191\t<type>(<scope>): <subject>\n192\t\n193\t<body>\n194\t\n195\t<footer>\n196\t```\n197\t\n198\t### Types\n199\t- `feat` - New feature (provider, tool, config option)\n200\t- `fix` - Bug fix\n201\t- `refactor` - Code restructuring without behavior change\n202\t- `test` - Test additions or modifications\n203\t- `docs` - Documentation only\n204\t- `chore` - Build, deps, config, tooling\n205\t- `perf` - Performance improvement\n206\t\n207\t### Scope\n208\tOptional but recommended. Use domain areas:\n209\t- `bedrock` / `vertex` / `openai` / `anthropic` / `mock` - Provider-specific\n210\t- `tools` - Tool registry and execution\n211\t- `orchestrator` - Chat orchestrator\n212\t- `transport` - Express/WebSocket\n213\t- `session` - Session management\n214\t- `types` - Type definitions\n215\t- `build` - Build/packaging\n216\t\n217\t### Examples\n218\t```\n219\tfeat(tools): add parallel tool execution batching\n220\tfix(bedrock): handle streaming timeouts gracefully\n221\trefactor(orchestrator): extract context building logic\n222\tdocs(providers): add Vertex AI implementation guide\n223\ttest(tools): add registry conflict detection\n224\t```\n225\t\n226\t### Rules\n227\t- Use imperative mood: \"add\" not \"added\" or \"adds\"\n228\t- Do not capitalize first letter of subject\n229\t- No period (.) at end of subject\n230\t- Limit subject to 50 characters\n231\t- Reference issues in footer: `Fixes #123`, `Relates to #456`\n232\t- Breaking changes in footer: `BREAKING CHANGE: description`\n233\t\n234\t## PR Requirements\n235\t\n236\t### Before Opening\n237\t1. Run full check suite and ensure all pass:\n238\t   ```bash\n239\t   npm run verify\n240\t   ```\n241\t2. Create a feature branch from `main`: `git checkout -b feat/description`\n242\t3. Commit with conventional format\n243\t\n244\t### PR Description Template\n245\t```markdown\n246\t## Description\n247\tBrief summary of changes and rationale.\n248\t\n249\t## Type of Change\n250\t- [ ] Feature\n251\t- [ ] Bug fix\n252\t- [ ] Breaking change\n253\t- [ ] Documentation\n254\t\n255\t## Changes\n256\t- Bullet list of concrete changes\n257\t- What was added/modified/removed\n258\t\n259\t## Testing\n260\t- How to test locally\n261\t- Any new test cases added\n262\t- Edge cases considered\n263\t\n264\t## Checklist\n265\t- [ ] Code follows style guide (eslint passes)\n266\t- [ ] No console.logs or debugging code\n267\t- [ ] TypeScript strict mode passes\n268\t- [ ] Tests added/updated for new behavior\n269\t- [ ] Documentation updated (README, specs/, adrs/, docs/)\n270\t- [ ] Spec `Status` rows re-evaluated if any spec gained or lost a test citation\n271\t- [ ] No new warnings in build output\n272\t```\n273\t\n274\t### Acceptance Criteria\n275\t- [ ] All GitHub Actions CI checks pass. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is three\n276\t      jobs - `verify` (sixteen steps, of which `npm run verify` reproduces\n277\t      fourteen), `build-chain` (audit, action pins) and `smoke` (packed tarball,\n278\t      both peer variants)\n279\t- [ ] Reviewed by somebody other than the author. `main` requires a pull request\n280\t      but is configured for zero required approvals and carries no `CODEOWNERS`,\n281\t      so this one is convention rather than a gate\n282\t- [ ] No merge conflicts\n283\t- [ ] Commit messages follow conventional format\n284\t- [ ] Tests cover new functionality (aim for >80% coverage on modified files)\n285\t- [ ] No dependencies added without discussion (especially AI provider SDKs)\n286\t- [ ] Breaking changes clearly documented and justified\n287\t- [ ] Public API changes described in `specs/`, not in comments (see Code Style)\n288\t\n289\t### Provider Implementation PRs\n290\tFor new provider support:\n291\t- [ ] Provider in `src/providers/<name>/` with index.ts exporting public API\n292\t- [ ] Implements `AIProvider` interface fully\n293\t- [ ] Handles streaming with `MessageChunk` protocol\n294\t- [ ] Tool/function calling mapped correctly\n295\t- [ ] Error handling with `AIError` type\n296\t- [ ] Tests for success and error scenarios\n297\t- [ ] Added to README.md provider table\n298\t- [ ] specs/hal-engine-providers/spec.md updated with implementation notes\n299\t\n300\t## Compliance Constraints\n301\t\n302\t### TypeScript\n303\t- `strict: true` in tsconfig.json is non-negotiable\n304\t- All public exports must be typed (no `any` in public APIs)\n305\t- Use `type` for type-only imports: `import type { ... } from '...'`\n306\t- Generics preferred over union types for reusability\n307\t\n308\t### Code Style\n309\t- ESLint config: `eslint.config.mjs` (flat config format)\n310\t- Max warnings: 0 (all warnings treated as errors)\n311\t- Comments: one line maximum (`re-lint/max-comment-lines`), and it counts a run\n312\t  of consecutive `//` lines as one comment. JSDoc is **not** exempt — a one-line\n313\t  `/** … */` passes, a multi-line block does not, which is exactly what\n314\t  `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` needs. Prose lives in `specs/`, `adrs/` and\n315\t  `docs/`. Exempt: tooling directives (`eslint-disable`/`eslint-enable`,\n316\t  `@ts-expect-error`/`@ts-ignore`/`@ts-nocheck`, triple-slash references,\n317\t  istanbul/c8 hints, `jscpd:ignore`, the shebang). An `eslint-disable` carries\n318\t  its reason after `--`\n319\t- Prettier config: `.prettierrc` (run before commit)\n320\t- Line length: 120 (`printWidth` in `.prettierrc`)\n321\t- Semicolons: Required\n322\t- Quotes: Single quotes (`singleQuote: true` in `.prettierrc`)\n323\t\n324\t### Testing\n325\t- Jest configuration: `jest.config.js`\n326\t- ts-jest for TypeScript support\n327\t- Minimum coverage: 70% lines for new code. That is review guidance; the gate is\n328\t  `coverageThreshold` in `jest.config.js`, set to what each path measured rather\n329\t  than to a flat 70, which had handed four groups an 18-30 point regression budget\n330\t- The floors ratchet: raise one when coverage rises, never lower one to make a\n331\t  change pass. A floor that moves down is a regression with the alarm switched off\n332\t- The exception is a change of instrument. An upgrade that counts branch points the\n333\t  old version never saw re-reads every floor at once, and a group whose denominator\n334\t  grew can fall while nothing about the suite changed. Settle it on covered/total\n335\t  absolutes rather than percentages, and record them where the floors live\n336\t- All public APIs require at least one test\n337\t- Async code must have proper await/done handling\n338\t\n339\t### Architecture Constraints\n340\t- **Layered structure mandatory** (types → providers → infrastructure → orchestration → transport)\n341\t- No circular dependencies between layers\n342\t- Providers must be pluggable (no hardcoded provider logic in orchestrator)\n343\t- Session store must be swappable (interface-based)\n344\t- Tools must be composable and testable in isolation\n345\t- WebSocket protocol defined in types/messages.ts (additive changes only for backward compatibility)\n346\t\n347\t### Dependency Management\n348\t- Core dependencies only (Express, ws, uuid, cookie, cors, cookie-parser)\n349\t- `@types/express`, `@types/node` and `@types/ws` are dependencies rather than\n350\t  devDependencies, because the emitted `.d.ts` files import `express`, `http`,\n351\t  `stream` and `ws`. Moving them back breaks a consumer's `tsc`, not ours\n352\t- Provider SDKs as optional peerDependencies\n353\t- No peer dependency version conflicts\n354\t- Security: `npm run check:audit` fails at `high` and above on the full installed\n355\t  tree. Moderate and low advisories do not block. An advisory that cannot be\n356\t  fixed yet goes in `.github/audit-acknowledgements.json` carrying advisory,\n357\t  package, reason, ISO expiry and who acknowledged it; an expired entry fails\n358\t  the gate, so an acceptance cannot become permanent by neglect\n359\t- License: Apache-2.0, declared in `package.json` and carried in `LICENSE`. No\n360\t  per-file licence headers: `re-lint/max-comment-lines` caps a comment at one\n361\t  line and exempts only tooling directives, so the thirteen-line Apache notice\n362\t  cannot go in a source file. Apache-2.0 recommends headers, it does not\n363\t  require them\n364\t\n365\t### Documentation Requirements\n366\t- A comment may span at most one line, JSDoc included — see Code Style. What a\n367\t  public function is and does belongs in `specs/`; what a signature is, the\n368\t  signature already says\n369\t- A one-line comment states the one constraint the code cannot show — \"why\", not\n370\t  \"what\". Anything longer goes to `specs/` or `adrs/`\n371\t- README.md kept in sync with actual features/examples\n372\t- specs/ directory is source of truth for architecture and protocols; adrs/ records decisions\n373\t- `.specify/spec.md` is the repo-level system spec. It is ingested by context\n374\t  tooling and served as authority, so a claim left stale there reaches every\n375\t  agent that assembles context for this repo. Keep it true when the manifest\n376\t  changes\n377\t- `CHANGELOG.md` at the repo root, Keep a Changelog 1.1.0, newest first under\n378\t  `## [Unreleased]`. Anything a consumer of the published package can observe\n379\t  gets an entry, written for somebody installing it rather than for somebody\n380\t  reading this repo's commit log. Enforced in CI: a pull request touching\n381\t  non-test files under `src/` fails unless it also touches `CHANGELOG.md`, and\n382\t  the `no-changelog` label is the deliberate escape hatch for a change nothing\n383\t  installable observes. Not derived from commit messages - a release note and a\n384\t  commit subject have different readers\n385\t\n386\t### Releasing\n387\t- Five human steps: bump `version` in `package.json` inside the pull request,\n388\t  merge it, draft a GitHub Release on `main` tagged `vX.Y.Z`, publish it, approve\n389\t  the staged version. Everything between the release and the approval is CI\n390\t- Release notes are hand-written on Keep a Changelog 1.1.0, not generated from\n391\t  commit subjects. The audience is somebody installing the package, who cannot\n392\t  act on `refactor(transport):` and needs to know what changed for them\n393\t- Rename `CHANGELOG.md`'s `## [Unreleased]` heading to the version being\n394\t  released in that same pull request, and open a fresh `## [Unreleased]` above it.\n395\t  `npm run check:version -- vX.Y.Z` fails if the heading does not name the version\n396\t- Run `npm run check:version -- vX.Y.Z` before publishing the release. A release\n397\t  and its tag can be deleted; an approved version cannot be replaced, and after\n398\t  72 hours cannot be withdrawn\n399\t- The tag must be `vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` and match `package.json` exactly.\n400\t  Prerelease tags are rejected because nothing passes `--tag`, so one would\n401\t  publish as `latest` and every plain `npm install` would resolve to it. A\n402\t  release ticked as a pre-release is refused before a runner starts\n403\t- Cut the release from a commit already merged to `main`. The workflow refuses a\n404\t  tag whose commit is not an ancestor of `origin/main`: a release can be drafted\n405\t  against any branch or commit, so without that check the protection on `main` is\n406\t  not the boundary the release rests on\n407\t- `.github/workflows/publish.yml` runs on a published release. A draft fires\n408\t  nothing, and `workflow_dispatch` on the release's tag is how a publish that\n409\t  failed after the release exists is re-run. It re-runs the version guard, the\n410\t  build-chain checks and the packed-tarball smoke test against the tagged commit,\n411\t  and refuses a pack list carrying any `*.test.*` entry, before anything is\n412\t  published. A tag points wherever its author chose, so a green run on `main` is\n413\t  not evidence about what is being released\n414\t- Authentication is npm Trusted Publishing over OIDC. There is no `NPM_TOKEN`\n415\t  secret, and the trusted publisher is registered against the workflow file's\n416\t  path - renaming or moving `publish.yml` stops publishing until it is\n417\t  re-registered\n418\t- The publish carries `--provenance`, which is available only because ADR-007\n419\t  makes the source repository public\n420\t- The publish is staged. The workflow puts the tarball, its dist-tag and its\n421\t  provenance on the registry, and the version stays uninstallable until a\n422\t  maintainer approves it with 2FA - the package page's Versions tab, or\n423\t  `npm stage list` then `npm stage approve <id>`. A staged version nobody\n424\t  approves reaches nobody, which is the closest thing to an undo that exists\n425\t  once a tag has fired\n426\t\n427\t### Breaking Changes\n428\t- MUST be discussed in issue before implementation\n429\t- MUST include migration guide in PR\n430\t- MUST increment MINOR version (semver)\n431\t- MUST update README quick start example if API changes\n432\t- MUST publish the migration guide as a `### Changed` entry in `CHANGELOG.md` as\n433\t  well as in the pull request: the PR is where reviewers read it, the changelog is\n434\t  where the consumer it affects reads it\n435\t- MUST add deprecation period (1 minor version) when possible. The unit is a\n436\t  published registry version and the audience is a consumer resolving one:\n437\t  a deprecation runs from the version that announces it to the version that\n438\t  removes the behaviour. A consumer on a git specifier is outside the clause -\n439\t  they pin a commit, so nothing announces anything to them and no minor\n440\t  elapses on their side\n441\t\n442\t### AI Provider Compliance\n443\t- No hardcoded API keys or secrets (use environment variables)\n444\t- Providers must handle rate limiting gracefully\n445\t- Tool calling must match JSON Schema spec\n446\t- Streaming must support cancellation/cleanup\n447\t- Usage tracking must be optional (SessionStore integration)\n448\t- Error messages must be actionable (not raw SDK errors)"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"faf5f8d3-0c23-4950-adf7-2f331a0b0b43","uuid":"efb8b4bd-2b41-4107-9170-93749248a4e8","timestamp":"2026-11-06T11:14:58.391Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/a58a74bb767d/workspace/target/AGENTS.md","content":"# AGENTS.md\n\n## Context Loading Order\n\nAgents should read files in this order to understand the HAL Engine codebase:\n\n1. **README.md** - Overview, features, quick start, and project structure\n2. **package.json** - Dependencies, scripts, tech stack validation\n3. **tsconfig.json** - TypeScript configuration and compilation targets\n4. **src/index.ts** - Public API exports and module surface\n5. **src/config.ts** - Main factory function `createHalEngine()` and configuration schema\n6. **src/types/** - Core type definitions (ai.ts, session.ts, messages.ts, auth.ts, sessionStore.ts)\n7. **src/providers/** - AI provider implementations (bedrock, vertex, openai, anthropic, mock)\n8. **src/infrastructure/** - Support layers (builders, stores, parsers)\n9. **src/orchestration/** - Business logic (chatOrchestrator, tools, context management)\n10. **src/transport/** - Server setup (Express app, WebSocket, routes)\n11. **specs/** - Normative specs (architecture, WebSocket protocol, providers, tool responses)\n12. **adrs/** - Architecture Decision Records\n13. **docs/** - How-to guides, coding practices, and research spikes under docs/spikes/\n14. **.specify/spec.md** - Repo-level system spec (exempt from the spec header table convention)\n\n## Workflow Commands\n\n### Development\n```bash\nnpm run dev          # Start example/server.ts with hot reload (tsx watch)\nnpm run typecheck    # Run TypeScript type checking without emitting\n```\n\n### Building\n```bash\nnpm run build        # Compile TypeScript to dist/ (tsc)\nnpm start            # Run compiled JavaScript from dist/index.js\n```\n\n### Testing\n```bash\nnpm test             # Run Jest test suite\n# Tests use ts-jest for TypeScript compilation\n# Configuration: jest.config.js\n```\n\n### Code Quality\n```bash\nnpm run eslint       # Run ESLint with max-warnings 0 (zero-tolerance)\nnpm run prettier     # Format all src/**/*.ts with Prettier\nnpm run prettier:check  # Check formatting without writing\n```\n\n### All Checks (Recommended Pre-Commit)\n```bash\nnpm run verify\n```\n\nThat is the blocking set `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `verify` job runs on every\npull request, in the same order, and `scripts/verify-script.test.ts` fails if the\ntwo stop agreeing - so this command cannot quietly become a subset of what CI\ndemands. Two gates in that job are missing from it because both diff against\n`origin/main` and neither runs the same way on a checkout: the spec anchor check\nand the changelog check.\n\nTwo further CI jobs are worth running locally before a release or a change that\ntouches packaging: `npm run check:build-chain` (audit and action pins) and\n`npm run smoke` (packs the tarball and installs it outside this tree).\n\n## Spec Header Table\n\nEvery `specs/*/spec.md` opens with its title, then a two-column header table,\nand carries a lead paragraph of prose before its first `##` section:\n\n```markdown\n# <spec title>\n\n| Field  | Value                  |\n| ------ | ---------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#<n> |\n| Status | Draft                  |\n\n<lead paragraph>\n```\n\nThe lead paragraph does not have to sit immediately after the table: a spec\ncarrying a metadata block of its own may place that block between the two. What\nthe convention requires is that prose, rather than a section heading, follows\nthe header material, and that the prose runs to at least 40 characters -\nanything shorter is reported as no lead paragraph at all.\n\n`Issue` names the issue the spec was written against, or `n/a` for the specs\nthat predate this convention.\n\n`Status` is one of `Draft`, `In Progress` or `Shipped`. It is not a mood, and\nit is not a claim about how finished the feature is: it is the spec's own\ntest-citation coverage. No testable statement cited is `Draft`, some cited is\n`In Progress`, all cited is `Shipped`. A spec describing shipped, working\nbehaviour still reads `Draft` until its statements cite the tests that\nvalidate them.\n\nA spec that should no longer be graded takes a terminal value instead. The\nparser buckets roughly sixteen labels into five buckets, and the two terminal\nones skip the coverage check whatever the citation count:\n\n| Written into the spec                                            | Bucket                       |\n| ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |\n| `Draft`                                                          | `draft`                      |\n| `In Progress`, `In Review`, `Planning`, `WIP`, `Proposed`        | `in-progress`                |\n| `Shipped`, `Implemented`, `Complete`, `Accepted`, `Done`, `Live` | `shipped`                    |\n| `Retired`, `Superseded`, `Removed`, `Deprecated`, `Obsolete`     | `retired` - skips the check  |\n| `Rejected`, `Abandoned`                                          | `rejected` - skips the check |\n\nPrefer the canonical `Draft` / `In Progress` / `Shipped` for a live spec; reach\nfor `Retired` or `Rejected` rather than leaving a superseded spec to claim a\ncoverage tier it will never earn.\n\nADRs do not carry this table. They declare their state as YAML frontmatter\n`status:` instead - `accepted` for a decision the code implements, `proposed`\nfor one it does not. That field does mean maturity, which the spec `Status` row\nexplicitly does not. The difference is deliberate: ADRs are exempt from the\ncoverage tier, because asking a decision record to cite a test for every\nstatement it makes is the wrong ask of a decision record.\n\n`.specify/spec.md` is exempt from this section. It is the repo-level system\nspec, it predates the convention, and its content is deliberately frozen -\ngiving it a header table and a lead paragraph would mean writing new prose into\na file that is meant to stay unchanged. The exemption covers the header table\nand the lead paragraph only: the Lore coverage job does scan\n`.specify/spec.md`, so link placement still applies to it.\n\n## Spec Test Links\n\nStatements in `specs/*/spec.md` cite their validating test in a trailing\nparenthetical at the end of the statement, where a list item counts as one\nstatement and a paragraph counts per sentence. Tests live beside their source\nin this repo, so the path points into `src/`:\n\n```markdown\n- All entries within a single assistant response are committed before the next\n  user message is processed\n  ([validated by: commits every entry before the next user message](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L42)).\n```\n\nThe citation carries the test's **name** as well as its line. The name is what the\nauthor cited; the line is a derived pointer, and it goes stale on its own - adding\nan import above a suite moves every test below it, and the citation lands on a\ndifferent, still-valid declaration without anything noticing. `npm run spec:names`\nholds each citation to the test it names, and `npm run spec:names:fix` repoints the\nline from the name. The `#Lnn` stays because the lint rule and the coverage job\nboth index by it; a citation without one marks the whole file covered rather than\none test.\n\nA citation counts as coverage only where it is trailing, and a test link placed\nanywhere else in the statement is a misplaced citation rather than a weaker one.\nUpstream `check:spec-links` reports it and exits 1, and the coverage job that\nconsumes these specs records nothing for it - so a spec can read as fully cited while its coverage is\nzero. A mid-statement link to something that is not a test file - a script, a\ndoc, another spec - is ordinary prose, is never reported, and was never coverage\nto begin with. A misplaced citation is fixed by moving it to the end of its\nstatement, never by deleting it.\n\nIn a test file the `#Lnn` anchor must land on the `it(`, `test(` or `describe(`\ndeclaration itself - not a blank line, not one holding only closing punctuation,\nand not a line inside the test body. A citation onto an `expect` renders and\nresolves like any other, and proves nothing: no reader can turn that line back\ninto the name of a case. `scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs` reports all four as\n`rotten` and exits 1 in both modes. A citation into a file that is not a test -\na workflow, a config, `package.json` - has no declaration to land on and is held\nonly to existing and carrying content.\n\nWhat no gate can catch is an anchor aimed at the wrong declaration: a real but\nunintended `it(` passes every check. The tell is that the test the statement\nmeant to cite goes on reading as uncited in the `require-spec-link` backlog.\n\nAnchors drift silently whenever a cited file gains or loses lines above them, so\nafter editing any cited file re-run `node scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs`\nrather than hand-correcting the numbers. That script cannot repair a spec absent\nfrom the base ref, since it has no baseline to translate from; on the branch that\nintroduces a spec, its anchors have to be corrected by hand.\n\nThe convention's tooling is wired up here: `npm run check:spec-links`, `npm run check:spec-status` and\n`node scripts/repoint-spec-anchors.mjs --check origin/main` all gate CI, and\n`re-lint/require-status-matches-coverage` derives each `Status` row from its\nspec's citations. `re-lint/require-spec-link` gates in `eslint.config.mjs`: every test cites a\nstatement, so an uncited new test fails the build. Its statement-side mirror,\n`require-statement-links`, runs at `warn` from `eslint.config.backlog.mjs` and\nreports without blocking - the severities the rule pair is designed for.\n\n## Commit Conventions\n\n### Format\nFollow conventional commits:\n```\n<type>(<scope>): <subject>\n\n<body>\n\n<footer>\n```\n\n### Types\n- `feat` - New feature (provider, tool, config option)\n- `fix` - Bug fix\n- `refactor` - Code restructuring without behavior change\n- `test` - Test additions or modifications\n- `docs` - Documentation only\n- `chore` - Build, deps, config, tooling\n- `perf` - Performance improvement\n\n### Scope\nOptional but recommended. Use domain areas:\n- `bedrock` / `vertex` / `openai` / `anthropic` / `mock` - Provider-specific\n- `tools` - Tool registry and execution\n- `orchestrator` - Chat orchestrator\n- `transport` - Express/WebSocket\n- `session` - Session management\n- `types` - Type definitions\n- `build` - Build/packaging\n\n### Examples\n```\nfeat(tools): add parallel tool execution batching\nfix(bedrock): handle streaming timeouts gracefully\nrefactor(orchestrator): extract context building logic\ndocs(providers): add Vertex AI implementation guide\ntest(tools): add registry conflict detection\n```\n\n### Rules\n- Use imperative mood: \"add\" not \"added\" or \"adds\"\n- Do not capitalize first letter of subject\n- No period (.) at end of subject\n- Limit subject to 50 characters\n- Reference issues in footer: `Fixes #123`, `Relates to #456`\n- Breaking changes in footer: `BREAKING CHANGE: description`\n\n## PR Requirements\n\n### Before Opening\n1. Run full check suite and ensure all pass:\n   ```bash\n   npm run verify\n   ```\n2. Create a feature branch from `main`: `git checkout -b feat/description`\n3. Commit with conventional format\n\n### PR Description Template\n```markdown\n## Description\nBrief summary of changes and rationale.\n\n## Type of Change\n- [ ] Feature\n- [ ] Bug fix\n- [ ] Breaking change\n- [ ] Documentation\n\n## Changes\n- Bullet list of concrete changes\n- What was added/modified/removed\n\n## Testing\n- How to test locally\n- Any new test cases added\n- Edge cases considered\n\n## Checklist\n- [ ] Code follows style guide (eslint passes)\n- [ ] No console.logs or debugging code\n- [ ] TypeScript strict mode passes\n- [ ] Tests added/updated for new behavior\n- [ ] Documentation updated (README, specs/, adrs/, docs/)\n- [ ] Spec `Status` rows re-evaluated if any spec gained or lost a test citation\n- [ ] No new warnings in build output\n```\n\n### Acceptance Criteria\n- [ ] All GitHub Actions CI checks pass. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is three\n      jobs - `verify` (sixteen steps, of which `npm run verify` reproduces\n      fourteen), `build-chain` (audit, action pins) and `smoke` (packed tarball,\n      both peer variants)\n- [ ] Reviewed by somebody other than the author. `main` requires a pull request\n      but is configured for zero required approvals and carries no `CODEOWNERS`,\n      so this one is convention rather than a gate\n- [ ] No merge conflicts\n- [ ] Commit messages follow conventional format\n- [ ] Tests cover new functionality (aim for >80% coverage on modified files)\n- [ ] No dependencies added without discussion (especially AI provider SDKs)\n- [ ] Breaking changes clearly documented and justified\n- [ ] Public API changes described in `specs/`, not in comments (see Code Style)\n\n### Provider Implementation PRs\nFor new provider support:\n- [ ] Provider in `src/providers/<name>/` with index.ts exporting public API\n- [ ] Implements `AIProvider` interface fully\n- [ ] Handles streaming with `MessageChunk` protocol\n- [ ] Tool/function calling mapped correctly\n- [ ] Error handling with `AIError` type\n- [ ] Tests for success and error scenarios\n- [ ] Added to README.md provider table\n- [ ] specs/hal-engine-providers/spec.md updated with implementation notes\n\n## Compliance Constraints\n\n### TypeScript\n- `strict: true` in tsconfig.json is non-negotiable\n- All public exports must be typed (no `any` in public APIs)\n- Use `type` for type-only imports: `import type { ... } from '...'`\n- Generics preferred over union types for reusability\n\n### Code Style\n- ESLint config: `eslint.config.mjs` (flat config format)\n- Max warnings: 0 (all warnings treated as errors)\n- Comments: one line maximum (`re-lint/max-comment-lines`), and it counts a run\n  of consecutive `//` lines as one comment. JSDoc is **not** exempt — a one-line\n  `/** … */` passes, a multi-line block does not, which is exactly what\n  `@param`/`@returns`/`@throws` needs. Prose lives in `specs/`, `adrs/` and\n  `docs/`. Exempt: tooling directives (`eslint-disable`/`eslint-enable`,\n  `@ts-expect-error`/`@ts-ignore`/`@ts-nocheck`, triple-slash references,\n  istanbul/c8 hints, `jscpd:ignore`, the shebang). An `eslint-disable` carries\n  its reason after `--`\n- Prettier config: `.prettierrc` (run before commit)\n- Line length: 120 (`printWidth` in `.prettierrc`)\n- Semicolons: Required\n- Quotes: Single quotes (`singleQuote: true` in `.prettierrc`)\n\n### Testing\n- Jest configuration: `jest.config.js`\n- ts-jest for TypeScript support\n- Minimum coverage: 70% lines for new code. That is review guidance; the gate is\n  `coverageThreshold` in `jest.config.js`, set to what each path measured rather\n  than to a flat 70, which had handed four groups an 18-30 point regression budget\n- The floors ratchet: raise one when coverage rises, never lower one to make a\n  change pass. A floor that moves down is a regression with the alarm switched off\n- The exception is a change of instrument. An upgrade that counts branch points the\n  old version never saw re-reads every floor at once, and a group whose denominator\n  grew can fall while nothing about the suite changed. Settle it on covered/total\n  absolutes rather than percentages, and record them where the floors live\n- All public APIs require at least one test\n- Async code must have proper await/done handling\n\n### Architecture Constraints\n- **Layered structure mandatory** (types → providers → infrastructure → orchestration → transport)\n- No circular dependencies between layers\n- Providers must be pluggable (no hardcoded provider logic in orchestrator)\n- Session store must be swappable (interface-based)\n- Tools must be composable and testable in isolation\n- WebSocket protocol defined in types/messages.ts (additive changes only for backward compatibility)\n\n### Dependency Management\n- Core dependencies only (Express, ws, uuid, cookie, cors, cookie-parser)\n- `@types/express`, `@types/node` and `@types/ws` are dependencies rather than\n  devDependencies, because the emitted `.d.ts` files import `express`, `http`,\n  `stream` and `ws`. Moving them back breaks a consumer's `tsc`, not ours\n- Provider SDKs as optional peerDependencies\n- No peer dependency version conflicts\n- Security: `npm run check:audit` fails at `high` and above on the full installed\n  tree. Moderate and low advisories do not block. An advisory that cannot be\n  fixed yet goes in `.github/audit-acknowledgements.json` carrying advisory,\n  package, reason, ISO expiry and who acknowledged it; an expired entry fails\n  the gate, so an acceptance cannot become permanent by neglect\n- License: Apache-2.0, declared in `package.json` and carried in `LICENSE`. No\n  per-file licence headers: `re-lint/max-comment-lines` caps a comment at one\n  line and exempts only tooling directives, so the thirteen-line Apache notice\n  cannot go in a source file. Apache-2.0 recommends headers, it does not\n  require them\n\n### Documentation Requirements\n- A comment may span at most one line, JSDoc included — see Code Style. What a\n  public function is and does belongs in `specs/`; what a signature is, the\n  signature already says\n- A one-line comment states the one constraint the code cannot show — \"why\", not\n  \"what\". Anything longer goes to `specs/` or `adrs/`\n- README.md kept in sync with actual features/examples\n- specs/ directory is source of truth for architecture and protocols; adrs/ records decisions\n- `.specify/spec.md` is the repo-level system spec. It is ingested by context\n  tooling and served as authority, so a claim left stale there reaches every\n  agent that assembles context for this repo. Keep it true when the manifest\n  changes\n- `CHANGELOG.md` at the repo root, Keep a Changelog 1.1.0, newest first under\n  `## [Unreleased]`. Anything a consumer of the published package can observe\n  gets an entry, written for somebody installing it rather than for somebody\n  reading this repo's commit log. Enforced in CI: a pull request touching\n  non-test files under `src/` fails unless it also touches `CHANGELOG.md`, and\n  the `no-changelog` label is the deliberate escape hatch for a change nothing\n  installable observes. Not derived from commit messages - a release note and a\n  commit subject have different readers\n\n### Releasing\n- Five human steps: bump `version` in `package.json` inside the pull request,\n  merge it, draft a GitHub Release on `main` tagged `vX.Y.Z`, publish it, approve\n  the staged version. Everything between the release and the approval is CI\n- Release notes are hand-written on Keep a Changelog 1.1.0, not generated from\n  commit subjects. The audience is somebody installing the package, who cannot\n  act on `refactor(transport):` and needs to know what changed for them\n- Rename `CHANGELOG.md`'s `## [Unreleased]` heading to the version being\n  released in that same pull request, and open a fresh `## [Unreleased]` above it.\n  `npm run check:version -- vX.Y.Z` fails if the heading does not name the version\n- Run `npm run check:version -- vX.Y.Z` before publishing the release. A release\n  and its tag can be deleted; an approved version cannot be replaced, and after\n  72 hours cannot be withdrawn\n- The tag must be `vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` and match `package.json` exactly.\n  Prerelease tags are rejected because nothing passes `--tag`, so one would\n  publish as `latest` and every plain `npm install` would resolve to it. A\n  release ticked as a pre-release is refused before a runner starts\n- Cut the release from a commit already merged to `main`. The workflow refuses a\n  tag whose commit is not an ancestor of `origin/main`: a release can be drafted\n  against any branch or commit, so without that check the protection on `main` is\n  not the boundary the release rests on\n- `.github/workflows/publish.yml` runs on a published release. A draft fires\n  nothing, and `workflow_dispatch` on the release's tag is how a publish that\n  failed after the release exists is re-run. It re-runs the version guard, the\n  build-chain checks and the packed-tarball smoke test against the tagged commit,\n  and refuses a pack list carrying any `*.test.*` entry, before anything is\n  published. A tag points wherever its author chose, so a green run on `main` is\n  not evidence about what is being released\n- Authentication is npm Trusted Publishing over OIDC. There is no `NPM_TOKEN`\n  secret, and the trusted publisher is registered against the workflow file's\n  path - renaming or moving `publish.yml` stops publishing until it is\n  re-registered\n- The publish carries `--provenance`, which is available only because ADR-007\n  makes the source repository public\n- The publish is staged. The workflow puts the tarball, its dist-tag and its\n  provenance on the registry, and the version stays uninstallable until a\n  maintainer approves it with 2FA - the package page's Versions tab, or\n  `npm stage list` then `npm stage approve <id>`. A staged version nobody\n  approves reaches nobody, which is the closest thing to an undo that exists\n  once a tag has fired\n\n### Breaking Changes\n- MUST be discussed in issue before implementation\n- MUST include migration guide in PR\n- MUST increment MINOR version (semver)\n- MUST update README quick start example if API changes\n- MUST publish the migration guide as a `### Changed` entry in `CHANGELOG.md` as\n  well as in the pull request: the PR is where reviewers read it, the changelog is\n  where the consumer it affects reads it\n- MUST add deprecation period (1 minor version) when possible. The unit is a\n  published registry version and the audience is a consumer resolving one:\n  a deprecation runs from the version that announces it to the version that\n  removes the behaviour. A consumer on a git specifier is outside the clause -\n  they pin a commit, so nothing announces anything to them and no minor\n  elapses on their side\n\n### AI Provider Compliance\n- No hardcoded API keys or secrets (use environment variables)\n- Providers must handle rate limiting gracefully\n- Tool calling must match JSON Schema spec\n- Streaming must support cancellation/cleanup\n- Usage tracking must be optional (SessionStore integration)\n- Error messages must be actionable (not raw SDK errors)","numLines":448,"startLine":1,"totalLines":448}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01RCfkaTpjW9FXBegPfWQvkr","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}
